in suitable coordinates near D. Consequently the standard almost complex structure in these coordinates is \(\omega_1\)-compatible on normal directions and can be extended globally.

1. an open neighborhood \(U\) of \(D\), a closed two-form \(\omega_D\) on \(U\), and a smooth path \(\omega_t\), \(0≤t≤1\), of symplectic forms on \(M\) in the cohomology class \([\omega]\), with \(\omega_0=\omega\), such that \((D,\omega_t)\) is a symplectic SNC divisor for all \(t\);
2. for every \(p∈D\) a coordinate chart \((z_1,...,z_n)\) on an open set \(U_p⊂U\) with \(D∩U_p=\{z_1...z_r=0\}\) and
\[
\omega_1|_{U_p}= \omega_{1,\mathrm{base}} + \sum_{i=1}^r \lambda_i(p?) dx_i∧dy_i
\]
block diagonal and \(dx_i∧dy_i\) positive on the normal subspaces; more precisely the standard complex structure on the span of \(dz_i\) is tamed by \(\omega_1\).

Let's state a lemma with proof:
**Lemma 1 (defining functions for a SNC divisor).** For a SNC divisor D with components \(D_1,\ldots,D_m\), there are an open neighborhood \(N\) of D and smooth complex-valued functions \(f_i\) on N (or on M, extended) such that:
1. \(f_i^{-1}(0)=D_i∩N\), \(df_i\) vanishes nowhere on \(D_i\).
2. If \(x∈N\), the set \(A(x)=\{i: df_i(x)\ne0\}\) has cardinal ≤n and \(\{df_i(x)\}_{i∈A(x)}\) are complex-linearly independent in the sense their real/imag differentials are independent.
3. For every \(p∈D\) there is a coordinate chart \(U\subset N\) and an index set A with \(p\in\{df_i\ne0\}\), \(D∩U=\{ \prod_{i∈A} f_i=0\}\), and (f_i)_{i∈A} can be completed to smooth complex coordinates.

Detailed proof of local coordinates from J:
Assume Theorem 1 gives a global J and path. At p∈D with components \(i_1,\dots,i_r\). By the normalization construction, there is a chart U with complex functions \(z_{i_1},\dots,z_{i_r}\) (from local defining functions) satisfying:
- \(z_i=0\) on D_i, dz_i independent.
- \(dz_i\circ J=i dz_i\) on U.
Then choose \(w_1,\dots,w_{n-r}\) local complex-valued functions on the slice \(S=\{z_i=0\}\), extended to U by making them constant along the normal directions. The collection is a coordinate system because the differentials dz_i are independent and dw independent on S. D∩U is union of D_i∩U, so product zero. The z_i are J-holomorphic. Done.
At p∉D, choose any chart disjoint from D and set r=0.
